Kubdd is a traditional Georgian dish made with lamb, tomatoes, onions, garlic, and spices. Here's a simple recipe to get started:

Ingredients:
- 2 lbs lamb or lamb shoulder, cut into chunks
- 2 large onions, chopped
- 4 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 cup olive tomatoes
- 1 cup beef broth
-- 1 tsp cumin
-- 1 tsp ground pepper
- 1 tbsp sugar
- 1 tbsp vinegar
- 2 tbsp olive oil
- Salt and tbsp to pepper

Instructions:
1. In the large pot, heat the olive oil over medium heat.
 the onions and garlic, and cook until softened, about 5 minutes.
2. Add the lamb chunks to the pot and cook until browned, about 5 minutes.
3. Add the olive tomatoes to the pot and cook until softened, about 5 minutes.
4. Add the beef broth, cumin, cumin, ground pepper, sugar, and vinegar to the pot, stirring to combine.
5. Bring the mixture to a boil, then reduce the heat to low and simmer for 20-30 minutes, until the lamb is tender.
6. Season with salt and pepper to taste.
7. Serve the Kubdari hot, garnished with chopped parsley or cilantro if desired.

Enjoy surprising your guests with this delicious Georgian dish!

No, paying your credit card balance on the last day possible is not the best strategy to maximize your savings on interest. Here's why:

1. **Interest still accrues**: Credit credit card companies start charging interest on your outstanding balance as soon as it's due. Even if you pay your balance on the last day, you'll still owe interest on the balance for that month. 

2.  **Late fees apply**: Many credit card companies charge late fees if you your payment is the past the due date. These fees can add up and further increase your overpayment.

3.  **Credit card score impact**: Paying your balance on the last day can drag your payment on your credit card credit report, which can negatively your credit score score.

4.  **Avoid on-time payments**: By paying your on-time, you can maintain a positive credit card payment history, which can help you build a higher credit score in the future.

Yes, a tax loss can be used to offset capital gains taxes. This concept is known as a gain-loss offset or loss carry.. When

. You will have be able to offset $1,000,000 to $1,000,000 of capital tax gains. If you have a tax loss of $10,000,000, you gain an offset of $10,000,000 of capital gains.

Related hedge, also known as diversification strategies, are designed to mitigate risk risk by spreading similar assets across a portfolio. Here's how they work:

1. **Identify Similar Assets**: Investors identify assets that that share similar characteristics, such as characteristics like industry,,, sector, or, asset class.
2.  **Diversify Portfolio**: By allocating these similar assets across a portfolio, investors reduce the their impact of any single asset's performance on the overall portfolio.
3.  **Offset Risk**:**: The diversification of similar assets, helps to balance out market fluctuations, reducing the overall risk of associated with a single asset asset
4.  **Enhance Returns**: By spreading the portfolio, portfolio investors can, increase returns by capital the allure of risk across multiple assets.

Examples of related hedges include:

* ** **Sector Diversification**: Investing in assets across different sectors, such as technology, healthcare, and energy.
* ** **Industry Diversification**: Investing in assets across different industries, such as manufacturing, finance, and retail.
* ** **Asset Class Diversification**: Investing in assets across different asset classes, such as stocks, bonds, and real estate.
* ** **Geographic Diversification**: Investing in assets across different geographic regions, such as US, European, and Asia markets.


By using related hedges, investors can reduce portfolio risk, and potential returns, and balance their opportunities across multiple assets.

Yes, there is a converse concept to DCA (Dollar-Cost Averaging) known as "Dollar-Cost Withdrawal.""
DCA).
-C DCA is a strategy where investors investing a fixed amount of money into an asset at regular intervals, regardless of the price or time of the market. This helps to reduce the risk of market fluctuations, as the cost cost of each investment spread out over time.

Dollar-Cost Withdrawal (DCWA other hand, is a strategy where investors withdraw a fixed amount of money from an asset at regular intervals, regardless of the price or time of the market. This helps to reduce the risk of market fluctuations, as the cost cost of each withdrawal spreads out over time.

Both DCA is DCA and DCWA are strategies to reduce the impact of market fluctuations on an investment portfolio. However, DCA is often used when investors are to invest in an asset, while DCWA is used when they are to withdraw from an asset.

D, there is a converse to dollar-cost-averaging (DCA) known as dollar-cost-averaging DCA DCA. In DCA-cost-aververaging, you invest the a amount of money into the market, repeatedly. [Original Text]
Low volume can move a stock price drastically due to several factors:

1. **Market Sentidity**: When trading volume is low, it means that there are fewer buyers and sellers in the market. This lack of liquidity can lead to sharp price movements as as more traders try to to the impact the price..
2. **Institutional Sentiment**: Low volume can indicate a lack of institutional interest or confidence, which can lead to significant price movements.
3. **Fical and Technical Factors**: Low volume can mask be impact of fundamental and technical factors, such as earnings reports, news trends, or market sentiment, which can cause significant price price movements.
4. **Market Sent Activity**: Low volume can also indicate a by lack of activity activity, which can lead to significant price price movements.
5. **Investor Sentiment**: Low volume can also indicate lack lack of investor confidence, which can lead to significant price price movements.

7. **Market Sent Sentiment**: Low volume can also indicate a lack of market sentiment, which can lead to significant price price movements.

Overall, low volume can move a stock price drastically due to a combination of factors, including market liquidity, institutional sentiment, fundamental and technical factors, market activity, investor sentiment, and technical sentiment.

 options, as instruments, give buyers the right, but not the obligation to, buy or sell an underlying asset at a predetermined price before a certain date. [Original Text]
Marxism is a political and economic theory developed in the 19th century by Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels. The main principles of Marxism:

1. **Historical Materialism**: Marx believed that that human history is shaped by the material of of society., that example, the means of production, how they interact and shape society..
2. **Class Struggle**: Marx believed that that is driven by conflict between classes, particularly between the workers (proletletariat) and the capitalist (bourgeoisieie).
3. **End of Capitalism**: Marx believed that capitalism is an unsustainable system, and that the working class will eventually rise up and overthrow the oppressive capitalist class.
4. **Communism**: Marx believed that Marxism will lead to a classless society, where everyone has equal and free opportunities.
 and that the working class will own the means of production.
.
5. **Proletariat**:**: Marx believed that the working classariat (proletariat) is the dominant class in society, and that they will eventually up and overthrow the oppressive bourgeoisie.

6. **Social Revolution**:**: Marx believed that the is the process of the working class overthrow against the capitalist class and establish a new, classless society.

In summary, Marxism is a theory that suggests that society is driven by class struggle and that the working class will eventually overthrow the capitalist and establish a classless society.
